style="height:0.345em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose nulldelimiter"></span></span><span class="mord">ℏ</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">ω</span></span></span></span> floor. The quanta are <strong>indistinguishable bosons</strong>: there is a single <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>a</mi><mo>†</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">a^\dagger</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8491em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">a</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8491em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">†</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>, and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>a</mi><mo>†</mo></msup><msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mi>n</mi></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(a^\dagger)^n</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0991em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">a</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8491em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">†</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose"><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">n</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> is automatically symmetric, so the state depends only on <em>how many</em>, never on <em>which</em>. This is the hinge of the whole course — in <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-i-just-in-time-physics/03-quantum-mechanics-as-oscillator-algebra/a-string-is-many-oscillators/" class="wiki-link">A string is many oscillators</a> each vibration mode gets its own <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>a</mi><mo>†</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">a^\dagger</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8491em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">a</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8491em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">†</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>, "<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">n</span></span></span></span> quanta in mode <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0315em;">k</span></span></span></span>" becomes "the string rings in its <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0315em;">k</span></span></span></span>-th harmonic <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">n</span></span></span></span> quanta deep," and those excitation numbers get reread as particles.</p>
<h2 id="step-5--no-states-hide-off-the-ladder">Step 5 — no states hide off the ladder</h2>
<p>The algebra shows every eigenstate lies on a ladder anchored at <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span>, but could there be a <em>second</em> normalizable bottom state, starting a parallel ladder? In the concrete Schrödinger representation, no. With <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>p</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mi>i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">ℏ</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi>d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\hat p=-i\hbar\,d/dx</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6944em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span the vacuum condition <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>a</mi><msub><mi>ψ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3011em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.0359em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">0</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span <p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>ω</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">ℏ</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mrow><mo fence="true">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">ℏ</mi><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>ω</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><mi>d</mi><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo fence="true">)</mo></mrow><msub><mi>ψ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mtext>  </mtext><mo>⟹</mo><mtext>  </mtext><msubsup><mi>ψ</mi><mn>0</mn><mo mathvariant="normal" lspace="0em" rspace="0em">′</mo></msubsup><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>ω</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">ℏ</mi></mfrac><mtext> </mtext><mi>x</mi><mtext> </mtext><msub><mi>ψ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">.</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\sqrt{\frac{m\omega}{2\hbar}}\left(x+\frac{\hbar}{m\omega}\frac{d}{dx}\right)\psi_0(x)=0 \;\Longrightarrow\; <p>This first-order ODE has a <em>one-dimensional</em> solution space,</p>
<p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>ψ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo separator="true">,</mo><mspace width="2em"></mspace><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mo fence="true">(</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>ω</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>π</mi><mi encoding="application/x-tex">\psi_0(x)=C\exp\!\left(-\frac{m\omega x^2}{2\hbar}\right),\qquad C=\left(\frac{m\omega}{\pi\hbar}\right)^{1/4},</annotation></semantics></math></span><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.0359em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">0</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> they form a complete orthonormal basis. No normalizable state hides off the single ladder.</p>
<h2 id="open-questions">Open questions</h2>
<ul>
<li>Completeness in Step 5 leans on the Sturm–Liouville theorem for a confining self-adjoint operator; that is mathematical background, not string-specific physics.</li>
<li>With several independent oscillators, different occupation lists can share the same total <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">N</span></span></span></span> — a degeneracy that is exactly the state-counting problem of <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-i-just-in-time-physics/06-partition-functions-and-state-counting/density-of-states/" class="wiki-link">Partition Functions and State Counting</a>.</li>
</ul>
<h2 id="exercise">Exercise</h2>
<p>The number operator "counts," but does it count linearly in energy? Compute the expectation and spread of energy in the state <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>a</mi><mo>†</mo></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">∣</mi><mn>0</mn><mo stretchy="false">⟩</mo><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">∣</mi><mn>1</mn><mo stretchy="false">⟩</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">a^\dagger|0\rangle=|1\rangle</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0991em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">a</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8491em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">†</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord">∣0</span><span class="mclose">⟩</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord">∣1</span><span class="mclose">⟩</span></span></span></span> directly, <em>without</em> using (2) — use only <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>a</mi><mo separator="true">,</mo><msup><mi>a</mi><mo>†</mo></msup><mo stretchy="false">]</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">[a,a^\dagger]=1</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0991em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mord mathnormal">a</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">a</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" Is <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∣</mi><mn>1</mn><mo stretchy="false">⟩</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">|1\rangle</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord">∣1</span><span class="mclose">⟩</span></span></span></span> an energy eigenstate (spread zero) or a superposition (spread nonzero)?</p>
<details class="callout callout-success">

style="height:0.345em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose nulldelimiter"></span></span><span class="mord">ℏ</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">ω</span></span></span></span>. The direct algebra reproduces the counting result, confirming <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">N</span></span></span></span> counts <em>linearly</em> — each rung adds exactly <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">ℏ</mi><mi>ω</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\hbar\omega</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6889em;"></span><span class="mord">ℏ</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">ω</span></span></span></span>, no more, no less.</p>
